City of South Lyon

Regular Council Meeting

August 10, 2020

Mayor Pelchat called the meeting to order at 7:30 p.m.

Mayor Pelchat led those present in the Pledge of Allegiance

Present: Mayor Pelchat, Councilmembers Dilg, Kennedy, Kurtzweil, Richards and Walton Councilmember Kivell joined the meeting at 7:35 p.m.

Also present: City Manager Zelenak, Chief Sovik, Chief Vogel, Attorney Hamameh, and Clerk/Treasurer Deaton

<a id="p3"></a>
*[Page 3 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/8-24-20%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202108310733160#page=3)* (read by OCR; check the original)

Nate Mack, the Director of the DDA stated most of the shop local, dine local banners are in the downtown. He stated there are some delays but we are hoping to get 3 more installed on Wednesday of this week. He stated he, Andrea Meyer, and Tina Archer to present to the Michigan Downtown Association on the 18th of this month regarding the task force campaign. He stated outside of the taskforce, he has been looking at advertising on Instagram and things like that. He stated some of the downtown businesses have over 1,000 followers. He stated we are waiting for the Patronicity funds from the County. He further stated he presented 5 letters of recommendation to local businesses seeking grant funding from the Heartbeat of America program. Councilmember Kivell asked if there is a banner in mind to adorn the corridor coming into a town. Mr. Mack stated they are working on getting a shop local and dine local banner.

## FIRE CHIEF REPORT

Chief Vogel stated Chief Weir is officially back to full duty today. He stated he is waiting for the official report on the ladder truck and what repairs are needed. He stated they passed their EMS inspection. He stated they also received word they received the grant for the training building for approximately $5,000.

## POLICE CHIEF REPORT

Chief Sovik stated there was an event in town called Voices over Choices and about 80 people attended at 4:00 p.m. today in town. He stated it lasted about an hour and they were expressing their displeasure of the School Board decision to have virtual learning. He then stated he is hoping to have more information to Council at the next meeting regarding the Comcast contracts to solve the issues they are having with the phone lines. He stated he received 2 checks for grants, one for $6,000 for in-car cameras, and $5,700 for the data storage project. He then stated he wanted to give Lt. Doug Baaki for the heavy position he carries at the department and he does a great job and he is comfortable leaving Lt. Baaki in charge when he isn't here.

## OLD BUSINESS

1. Request for approval to submit resolution approving proposed charter amendment ballot proposition to the County Clerk

Attorney Hamameh stated Council authorized language to be presented to the Attorney General and the Governor's Office for approval before it is placed on the ballot. She stated the Attorney General's office has given tentative approval. She stated she spoke with someone from the Governor's Office and they are expecting the Governor to sign off on this Wednesday. The deadline is Tuesday the 11th, so she is suggesting we move forward with this because it is highly unlikely that the Governor will disagree with the language. Councilmember Dilg asked what will happen if it is rejected after it is submitted. Attorney Hamameh stated the AG's office and the Governor's office was surprised to hear that Oakland County said the deadline to submit or pull is 4:00 p.m. due to the fact there are many communities looking to do ballot propositions that aren't as far ahead as we are.

## CM 8-5-20 MOTION TO AUTHORIZE SUBMISSION OF BALLOT PROPOSAL RESOLUTION

Motion to authorize submission of the Resolution approving proposed Charter amendment ballot proposal to the County Clerk on Augusts 11, 2020

ROLL VOTE:

Dilg- Yes

Walton-Yes

Kurtzweil- Yes

Kennedy-Yes

Kivell- Yes

Richards- Yes

## NEW BUSINESS

1. Agreement for 390 S Lafayette Street preliminary geotechnical investigation (soil borings) with G2 Consulting Group of Troy, Michigan

Nate Mack, DDA Director stated the City of South Lyon has identified the parcel located at 390 S Lafayette St as a site for potential development in the master plan. He stated part of the development process is determining if whatever the soil is made of, if it can basically handle the weight of a building. He stated the company G2 will be drilling two holes that go 60 feet down and tests the soil at different intervals. He stated G2 was the lowest of the two bids we received. Councilmember Richards stated because it is municipally owned, we have to go through with this, and this is a cheap price, there is a lot of peat down there and the grade of the peat is top grade. It may be to our advantage to get the solid dirt for our own use in the future.

He stated the previous quote from Jim's Amish Structures included two structures in order to have #6 well matched to the #4 well site. He then stated this item can be purchased utilizing account 592.556.931. Councilmember Kivell stated he is hoping we can modify the buildings with a steel door to make them more secure. Superintendent Varney stated they are currently working on that.

He stated the valve will allow the plants pumps to cycle in an automated mode and the water cannot be pumped properly without this valve working. He then stated this can be purchased out of account 592.556.801 Councilmember Kennedy thanked Doug for answering his questions over the weekend. ## BUDGET

Mayor Pelchat stated we have been trying to work with the schools with replacing the media equipment in the media room, but we haven't been successful as of yet. Some of the equipment is very old and will be hard to replace, and hard to find someone that knows how to work on them. He then asked the City Manager to check in with the schools and speak with them about their bond issue passing and if they will be interested in trying to work together to replace the equipment. Councilmember Kivell stated that is a good suggestion.

## MANAGER'S REPORT

City Manager Zelenak stated he wanted to remind everyone about the zoning rewrite and he hopes Council has been able to look through the changes the Planning Commission is requesting. City Manager Zelenak reminded everyone how important the census is and he hopes everyone will be counted. He then stated there has been an increase in Covid cases reported by the health department but no other information has been offered other than they are trying to attribute them to a few things that happened in the Township. He stated he has been working on a draft for the RFP for the building at 318 W Lake St. He stated the final development agreement approval will come before Council for approval. He stated the agreement reflects the requirement to build a home that is conducive with the surrounding homes. He stated the development packet will be available on our website. He stated he has been working on our capital improvement plan and in particular, sidewalks and railway improvements. He is hoping the Township will partner with us in making some of the improvements.

Councilmember Dilg asked how the agreement worked with the County running our absentee ballots. Clerk Deaton stated it worked out really well. They picked up all absentee ballots that were returned to the Clerk's office around 5:30 previous to Election Day. Two of our Election Inspectors met at the County around 6:15 on Election Day for preparation to begin processing ballots. The County reported they processed 50,000 absentee ballots on Election Day and they didn't have any issues.

Councilmember Kivell asked if the City Manager has spoken with any of the residents near 318 W Lake Street. City Manager Zelenak stated he has spoken with a few of the residents that were concerned that someone would build something that doesn't fit in with the surrounding neighborhood. He stated everyone 8/11/20

<a id="p6"></a>
*[Page 6 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/8-24-20%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202108310733160#page=6)* (read by OCR; check the original)

seems very happy about the plan. Councilmember Richards asked what if the prospective bidder wants to build a house, and live there. That is different than putting it on the market. City Manager Zelenak stated he doesn't have a problem if someone wants to build there and use it for their own purpose.

## PUBLIC COMMENT- None

## COUNCIL COMMENTS

Councilmember Richards stated he ran into Pipetek in town and he spoke with them. He stated he asked if they were going to be using cameras as they go through the lines and they said they did. He further stated they were very friendly and he let them know about the drainage issues the City has always had.

Councilmember Dilg thanked Josie and Joe for all their time on the Cultural Arts Commission. She then welcomed Dayna Johnston to the Cultural Arts Commission. She then thanked Lisa Deaton and all the office staff for all their work during the Election. She stated she can't imagine all the hard work it was with the increase in the number of absentee ballots along with the issues of the ballots not being delivered. She stated many people have told her about the friendliness of the staff when they came into the office to report they spoiled their ballot, or requesting a ballot. She then commended the participation of everyone at the event that was held in South Lyon today, and she can't imagine having small children and making the decisions parents are having to make regarding their schooling. She then stated she feels for the school board members that are in a school board meeting tonight making a tough decision. She stated they are reading over 70 emails and her heart goes out to them.

Councilmember Kurtzweil stated she spoke with Josie and Joe and she thanked them personally for all their time they spent on the Cultural Arts Commission. She then thanked the City for moving so quickly with appointing Dayna so the commission will again have a quorum. She then stated she had some friends that attended the voices for choices event and that is a big thing, we have choices. She stated the frustrating thing is they have to deal with people that have two different ideologies but people respond better when they have choices. She further stated when she is given a choice, things usually work out ok. She then stated we have to learn to live with Covid-19. It isn't going to go away; we need to continue to wash our hands and wear masks. Masks mean freedom. We will get through this.

Councilmember Walton thanked Josie and Joe for all the amazing things they have done for our City and they will be missed. Dayna will be a great asset on the Commission and she can't wait to see the great things she will bring.

Councilmember Kennedy reminded everyone to complete their census questionnaire. The deadline for being counted is September 30 $ ^{th} $. Please go to 2020census.gov and complete it today. He then asked everyone to please check with your neighbors and friends to remind them to do it as well. Time is running out to be counted. The amount of money that is allocated to the city based on the census will be even more critical for the next 10 years considering the current economic conditions and situation.

Councilmember Kivell stated the last numbers he seen was that the City is missing 18% of the residents. He further stated 18% is a huge number and everyone needs to be counted to help address some of the road issues. Councilmember Kivell stated he heard the City is trying to assist with getting low branches out of roadways and sidewalks. He stated when you are riding your bike and you get whipped by branches because they hang over the sidewalk too much isn't acceptable. He further stated our Ordinance

Officer seems to be working on this and he is happy about that. He further stated he hopes homeowners will step up to the plate, and keep their branches and bushes trimmed out of the right of way.

Mayor Pelchat stated the Ordinance Officer does a great job and it is a thankless job. Mayor Pelchat thanked Josie and Joe for all the things they did for the Cultural Arts Commission. They have been an incredible asset for them. He then said Dayna Johnston will be a great fit for the City. He stated he stopped in at the polls to say hi to everyone and he thanked all the inspectors and the voters. He then stated the flags are up as well as in the township. He stated it is a great reminder to keep our dollars in our community.

# AGENDA NOTE New Business: Item # /

MEETING DATE: August 24, 2020

## PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Fire Chief Robert Vogel

AGENDA TOPIC: Acceptance of the State of Michigan Training Council burn building grant and purchase of the burn building.

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: SLFD has received a grant from the State of Michigan Training Council for the amount of $4,700.00 ($5,018.00) to purchase two shipping containers to be utilized as a firefighter training building.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: MFFTC Grant award letter, SLFD submitted application, three (3) submitted quotes, container picture, and WWTP map.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve/do not approve the receipt of the State of Michigan Training Council burn building grant and purchase of the burn building.

## SUGGESTED MOTION:

Motion by _____, supported by _____ to approve the grant from State of Michigan Training Council for the amount of $4,700.00, to be deposited to 101-000.000-698.600 (Fire Grant Account) to purchase two shipping containers for the purpose of a firefighter's burn building from Capital Equipment Account 101-335.000-978.000.

## Funding and Scheduling:

Approved grant funding will be transferred to the county of the primary applicant via the SMOKE program. Upon receipt of the funds, a course manager must enter the course and/or equipment/prop to allocate the funding. Please be sure to use the appropriate course code (i.e. A25D, Q02B, etc.) and  $ \underline{\text{schedule the course by March 31, 2020}} $. If your application included an equipment/prop purchase, please enter course code M11A and PIN 784250 as the instructor of record.

## Bid Process for Equipment/Props:

The following are the requirements for purchases using State of Michigan funds. Please also ensure that you follow all local/department purchasing rules when purchasing the equipment/props.

## Equipment/Props $2,500 to $9,999:

1. When the cost of the equipment/props exceeds $2,500 and is up to $9,999, three (3) price quotes are required.

2. The three (3) quotes will need to be submitted to the LARA-BFS-Grants@michigan.gov inbox with a justification on why you selected the chosen vendor. Please indicate the course code and the name of the county purchasing the equipment/prop in the subject line of your email.

3. Your region coordinator will contact you within three (3) business days of receipt of your email to the LARA-BFS-Grants@michigan.gov inbox with the three (3) quotes and the justification for the chosen vendor.

4. Once your region coordinator has evaluated your quotes, you will receive an approval or denial email.

5. Once you have received approval from your region coordinator, you may purchase the equipment/props.

<a id="p12"></a>
*[Page 12 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/8-24-20%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202108310733160#page=12)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## Equipment/Props $10,000 or more:

1. When the cost of the equipment/prop is $10,000 or more the request must go out for official request for proposal (RFP). Anyone can bid and the RFP must remain open for at least 14 calendar days.

2. A copy of the RFP, the results of the RFP and a justification on why you selected the chosen vendor must be emailed to the LARA-BFS-Grants@michigan.gov inbox. Please indicate the course code and the name of the county purchasing the equipment/prop in the subject line of your email.

3. Your region coordinator will contact you within three (3) business days of receipt of your email to the LARA-BFS-Grants@michigan.gov inbox with the RFP, bids and justification for the chosen vendor.

4. Once your region coordinator has evaluated your bids you will receive an approval or denial email.

5. Once you have received approval from your region coordinator you may purchase the equipment/props.

As a reminder, all courses using FY20 funds must be finalized by September 30, 2020. All equipment/props using FY20 funds must also be received by September 30, 2020. If you are unable to complete the process by the required deadline, please advise your region coordinator as soon as possible so that FY20 funds may be transferred to the next applicant on the funding list as approved by the MFFTC at the February 11, 2020 meeting.

If you have questions, please contact your region coordinator.

Dan Hammerberg

Region 1 Coordinator

Phone: 906-399-4399

Email: hammerbergd@michigan.gov

*[Page 14 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/8-24-20%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202108310733160#page=14)* (read by OCR; check the original)

1. If awarded the MFFTC training grant we would purchase two 8x40 shipping containers and build a multi-use training facility that would be used for live fire training, search and rescue drills, thermal imaging, forcible entry, and countless other trainings. The facility would be utilized by multiple departments.

2. Requested funding: $5,018.00. Cost of each container is $2,350 with $318 delivery and set-up.

3. South Lyon is a city in southwestern Oakland County, in metropolitan Detroit. The South Lyon Fire Department is a paid-on-call fire department that has served the City of South Lyon since 1893. The department is led by a part-time fire chief who also serves as the main administrator. Two on-call employees staff the fire station on weekdays from 7:00 AM to 5:00 PM and Saturdays from 10:00 AM to 8:00 PM. All other hours are exclusively on-call. Due to its geographic location in the southwest corner of Oakland County, the South Lyon Fire Department has automatic and mutual aid agreements with departments in Washtenaw and Livingston Counties and is part of the Mutual Aid Box Alarm System (MABAS) section 3201 in Oakland County. All fire suppression staff are trained in Firefighter I & II and all fire officers are trained at a minimum in Fire Officer I & II through the Michigan Fire Fighters Training Council. Additionally officers must complete 50 online hours of Blue Card Command training with several officers having completed the live simulation lab becoming Blue Card Certified. The department is also trained and equipped to a confined space operations level to provide stand-by and rescue support to the city's Department of Public Works and wastewater treatment facility. Currently the closest training facility is 10 minutes outside the city and requires training nights to be split up into multiple nights to ensure the city maintains emergency coverage. The proposed multi-use training facility would be in the city and be utilized by South Lyon Fire and the multiple departments that we regularly assist. The realistic setting would provide for more specialized drills and evolutions allowing us to learn new firefighting skills, hone previously taught skills, and provide a medium for the development of training for future CE's required by Firefighter Training Council Act PA 291.

The South Lyon Fire Department has been researching a multi-use training facility since early 2019. We have secured a location in the city and preparing the land will be completed by South Lyon DPW employees. All other interior preparation will be done by fire staff volunteering their time.

We have not pursued any other grants for this project. We have, however reached out to local businesses for material donations including wood studs and drywall for interior building construction.

# AGENDA NOTE New Business: Item #2

MEETING DATE: August 24, 2020

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: City Manager/Police Chief/Fire Chief

AGENDA TOPIC: New Phone and Internet Provider

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The Public Safety Complex (Police and Fire Buildings) currently has a phone system that is very unreliable and has consistently failed, leaving citizens unable to call their local police and fire departments for assistance. The current system is made up of Avaya phone equipment and phone switch. The maintenance for the entire system is handled by CBTS, Windstream and AT&T. At the end of June 2020 Windstream informed us that they could no longer assist us when our phone system went down due to our phone switch needing to be replaced. We then had an independent contractor come out to confirm Windstream's diagnosis.

We contacted three different vendors (Comcast, Teoma Systems, and Verizon) to quote a new phone switch, equipment and maintenance service. The only phone provider that was CJIS compliant was Comcast. The police department must be in compliance with CJIS (Criminal Justice Information System) regarding the very sensitive information handled on a daily basis. Being CJIS compliant means that our phone lines will be encrypted and any conversations/faxes/emails will comply with federal regulations.

The main benefits that Comcast offers are CJIS compliance, maintenance is monitored 24/7 and will more than likely be discovered and repaired without us knowing there is a failure. There is not a separate costly maintenance agreement because it's included in the contract. The new phone hardware is easily replaced at no cost to us should it fail. The phone hardware is remotely updated when new software is available. Department Heads or their designee will be able to monitor and make adjustments to the setup through a phone app.

City Hall, P.D. and F.D. were planning to research new phone vendors during FY 20/21 for the FY 21/22 budget. Due to the unforeseen failure of the phone switch at the Public Safety Complex, this process must be expedited. To be economically responsible, Comcast offered a monthly discounted rate if we included the additional number of phones needed at City Hall who is experiencing less severe problems with their phone system.

The City Attorney suggested that any indemnification clause within Comcast's contract have added language stating “To the extent permitted by law and without waiving governmental immunity, the Customer agrees...” Comcast has agreed that this is a

<a id="p21"></a>

reasonable request but an updated contract will not be ready by the agenda submission date.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Business voice edge agreement, City of South Lyon design, phone comparison, photo of equipment, Ethernet explanation, Master service agreement, phone features, Sales order agreement, Verizon quote.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve/Do Not Approve Comcast phone and internet service provider contracts and agreements.

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by _____, supported by _____ to approve the Comcast phone and internet service provider contracts and agreements.

<table><tr><td>Company</td><td>Pros</td><td>Cons</td><td>Cost</td></tr><tr><td>Comcast</td><td>1.) Dedicated Fiber Network (Very reliable and little to no maintenance)2.) CJIS Compliant3.) We own the equipment2.) Lower cost due to Verizon using a 3rd party vendor to maintain the network and installation process.3.) Offers additional modems as a backup in case the internet fails. # Ethernet Dedicated Network Service

BEYOND FAST

Comcast Business Ethernet Dedicated Internet (EDI) Service is a simple, reliable and more flexible option to traditional private line dedicated Internet access services, providing higher bandwidth and increased efficiencies.

Ethernet Dedicated internet provides an Ethernet Virtual Connection (EVC) from the customer premises location to a Comcast Internet Point of Presence (POP) router. Our Ethernet interface enables compatibility with your LAN.

*[Image or figure on this page; see the original.]*

For enterprises that need the most bandwidth and the fastest connection providing a continuous link between their existing LAN and the public Internet, there is no better way to connect than with Comcast Business Ethernet Dedicated Internet.

Ethernet Dedicated internet is offered in 10Mbps/100Mbps, 1Gbps and 10Gbps Ethernet User Network Interface (UNI) in speed increments from 2Mbps to 10Gbps subject to available capacity.

For more information or a free consultation, contact your local Enterprise Account Executive.

## Symmetrical Connectivity

Symmetrical dedicated internet bandwidth configurable from 2Mbps to 10Gbps in 1Mbps increments

## IP Addresses

business.comcast.com/ethernet

Static IP addresses assigned based on American Registry for Internet Numbers (ARIN) guidelines and customer justification

## Domain Name System

Includes primary and secondary DNS service. Comcast will assist customers in transferring existing domain names.

## Border Gateway Protocol Routing

Optional BGP setup to facilitate multihoming between multiple ISP networks

## Web-Based Reporting

Connectivity statistics for the previous day, seven-day or thirty-day period can be viewed through a secure web portal

## Business Support

24/7/365 network monitoring and support through our Business Network Operations Center (BNOC)

This Master Service Agreement ("Agreement") sets forth the terms and conditions under which Comcast Cable Communications Management, LLC and its operating affiliates ("Comcast") will provide communications and other services ("Services") to the above Customer. The Agreement consists of this fully executed Master Service Agreement Cover Page ("Cover Page"), the Enterprise Services General Terms and Conditions ("General Terms and Conditions"), any written amendments to the Agreement executed by both parties ("Amendments"), the Product-Specific Attachment for the applicable Services ("PSA(s)") and each Sales Order accepted hereunder ("Sales Orders"). In the event of any inconsistency among these documents, precedence will be as follows: (1) this Cover Page, (2) General Terms and Conditions, (3) PSA(s) and (4) Sales Orders. This Agreement shall be legally binding when signed by both parties and shall continue in effect until the expiration date of any Service Term specified in a Sales Order referencing the Agreement, unless terminated earlier in accordance with the Agreement.

The Customer referenced above may submit Sales Orders to Comcast during the Term of this Agreement ("MSA Term"). After the expiration of the initial MSA Term, Comcast may continue to accept Sales Orders from Customer under the Agreement, or require the parties to execute a new MSA.

The Agreement shall terminate in accordance with the General Terms and Conditions. The General Terms and Conditions and PSAs are located at https://business.comcast.com/terms-conditions-ent (or any successor URL). Use of the Services is also subject to the High-Speed Internet for Business Acceptable Use Policy ("AUP") located at http://business.comcast.com/customer-notifications/acceptable-use-policy (or any successor URL), and the High-Speed Internet for Business Privacy Policy ("Privacy Policy") located at http://business.comcast.com/customer-notifications/customer-privacy-statement (or any successor URL). Comcast may update the General Terms and Conditions, PSAs, AUP and Privacy Policy from time to time upon posting to the Comcast website.

Services are only available to commercial customers in wired and serviceable areas in participating Comcast systems (and may not be transferred). Minimum Service Terms are required for most Services and early termination fees may apply. Service Terms are identified in each Sales Orders, and early termination fees are identified in the applicable Product Specific Attachments.

# AGENDA NOTE New Business: Item # 3

MEETING DATE: August 24, 2020

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: Police Chief/Fire Chief

AGENDA TOPIC: Phone Infrastructure Contract from Challenger Technologies

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: Challenger Technologies will be contracted to provide the infrastructure of fiber optic lines to each building within the public safety complex. This project was not budgeted for the FY 20/21, however, due to recent unforeseen phone equipment failure with the phone switch, we will need to move this project forward. The phone switch currently handles the phone system for all three buildings within the Public Safety complex. Payment for this project will be brought forth as a budget amendment requesting funds from line item 100-330-970 Capital Outlay (Police) and 100-335-970 Capital Outlay (Fire) in a December 2020 City Council meeting.

This project will be needed to connect all three buildings within the Public Safety Complex. This will afford us the ability to own the underground fiber optic lines which will be more secure and less vulnerable to outages as opposed to having the fiber optic lines in an aerial setting. Since we have chosen to go with Comcast, who utilizes fiber optic lines, this will save us approx. $1500 per month by having all three buildings connected by one line as opposed to one line per building.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Challenger construction agreement, exhibit A, exhibit B, fiber comparison, certificate of liability insurance, and sketch.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Approve/Do Not Approve the Challenger Technologies contract in the amount of $12,128.

SUGGESTED MOTION: Motion by _____, supported by _____ to approve the Challenger Technologies contract in the amount of $12,128.

# CONTRACT FOR FIBER OPTIC CABLE INSTALLATION

THIS CONTRACT FOR FIBER OPTIC INSTALLATION ("Contract"), IN CONSIDERATION OF the covenants and conditions hereafter expressed in this "Contract" is made this ___ day of August 2020, between the City of South Lyon, whose address is 335 South Warren Street, South Lyon, MI 48178 ("Owner") and Challenger Technologies, LLC, whose address is 2501 Precision Street, Jackson, MI 49202 ("Contractor").

## COVENANTS AND CONDITIONS:

## 1 )  $ \underline{\text{Scope of Work ("WORK")}} $

a) This contract is for the fiber optic cable installation on the Owner's property as more specifically described in attached Exhibit A (the "Work"). Any changes to the Work shall be set forth in writing signed by both parties.

## 2 )  $ \underline{\text{Payment Terms}} $

a) The Owner agrees to pay the Contractor an amount not to exceed Twelve Thousand One Hundred Twenty-Eight Dollars ($12,128.00) for the completion of the Work by Contractor. Payment shall be made upon verification of Work completion to the satisfaction of the Owner and approval of invoices received by Owner. Such payment shall be made not more than thirty (30) days after invoice approval by Owner. All payments to Contractor shall be submitted by mail at Contractor's address first listed above, unless Contractor provides written notice of a change in the address to which such payments are to be sent.

b) The Contractor may make application for progress payments to the Owner at any time during the project, which Owner my grant in its sole discretion.

c) The progress payment application to the Owner shall include an original of the following:

i) Invoice describing the Work that was performed; and

ii) The payment amount requested; and estimate of percentage of the Work completed.

## 3 )  $ \underline{\text{Time of Completion}} $

a) both parties agree that the Work shall commence on or after August 17, 2020, but no later than fourteen (14) days after the execution of this Contract (the "commencement date"), and substantial completion of the Work shall be completed within thirty (30) days of the commencement date.

b) The Contractor shall employ persons of competence and skill to complete the project within thirty (30) days of the agreed upon commencement date.

c) If the Contractor fails to complete the Work as agreed herein, the Owner may declare the Contractor in default by providing written notice to Contractor by registered mail.

i) If Contractor fails to remedy such default within fifteen (15) days of such notice, this Contract shall terminate, and Owner shall have the right to select a substitute Contractor.

ii) If the expense of completing the Work exceeds the unpaid balance on this "Contract", the Contractor shall pay the difference to the Owner within thirty (30) days of its invoicing.

d) In agreeing to complete the Work by the agreed upon Time of Completion, Contractor has taken into consideration and made allowance for ordinary delays, and hindrances incident to such work, whether growing out of delays of common carriers, delays in securing material or workers, weather conditions, changes, omissions, alterations, or otherwise. During the course of the project, if the Contractor determines that completion cannot occur during the timeframe given, Contractor will make immediate notification to Owner in order to attempt to arrive at a mutually accepted extension. The decision to grant an extension or to declare a default, as described in subparagraph (c) above, is in the sole discretion of the Owner.

## 4 )  $ \underline{\text{Confidentiality}} $

a) Each party agrees not to use any Confidential Information of the other party for any purpose except to evaluate and engage in the Work.

b) Except as required by law, each party agrees not to disclose any Confidential Information of the other party, except that a receiving party may disclose the other party's Confidential Information to those employees of the receiving party who are required to have the information to evaluate or engage in the Work.

## 5 )  $ \underline{\text{Availability of Equitable Relief}} $

a) Each party understands and agrees that its breach or threatened breach of this Contract will cause irreparable injury to the other party and that money damages will not provide an adequate remedy for such breach or threatened breach, and both parties hereby agree that, in the event of such a breach or threatened breach, the non-breaching party will also be entitled, without the requirement of posting a bond or other security, to equitable relief, including injunctive relief and specific performance.

b) The parties' rights under this Contract are cumulative, and a party's exercise of one right shall not waive the party's right to assert any other legal remedy.

<a id="p46"></a>
*[Page 46 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/8-24-20%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202108310733160#page=46)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## 6 )  $ \underline{\text{Waivers}} $

a) No provision of this Contract may be waived except by a writing executed by each party or an authorized representative of each party.

b) A party's failure to enforce any provision of this Contract shall neither be construed as a waiver of the provision nor prevent the party from enforcing any other provision of this Contract.

## 7 ) Modification of Contract

a) Any modification of this Contract or additional obligations assumed by either party in connection with this Contract shall be binding only if placed in writing and signed by each party or an authorized representative of each party.

8) Guarantee. Neither the verification and approval of the final invoice, nor the making of the final payment by the Owner to the Contractor shall relieve the Contractor of the responsibility for faulty materials or workmanship discovered with one (1) year after the date of final payment for the Work; and the Contractor shall promptly replace any such defects without cost to the Owner.

9) Independent Contractor. The parties to the Contract intend that the relationship between them created by this Contract is that of a service provider and service purchaser. It is expressly agreed, understood and intended that no employee-employer relationship shall exist or be established and that Contractor is an independent contractor who has been retained to render services to the Owner to achieve specific results in exchange for specified recompense. As an independent contractor, Contractor expressly agrees that: (a) In the performance of this Contract, the relationship of Contractor to Owner shall be that of an independent contractor and not that of an employee or agent of the Owner, and neither Contractor, nor any agent, employee or agent of, or a servant to, the Owner; (b) Contractor will be solely responsible for payment of salaries, wages, and other compensation for its employees and agents; (c) Neither the Contractor nor any officer, agent, employee or subcontractor of the Contractor shall be eligible for coverage under or eligible to receive benefits of the Owners' Workers' compensation, unemployment or health insurance, pension plans or other benefit plans; (d) Contractor is and shall perform under this Contract as an independent contractor, and no liability or responsibility with respect to benefits of any kind, including without limitation, medical/health benefits, Workers' compensation, pension rights, or other rights or liabilities arising out of or related to a contract for hire or employer/employee relationship shall arise or accrue to either party as a result of the performance of this Contract; and (e) Contractor, as an independent contractor, is not authorized to enter into or sign any agreements on behalf of the Owner.

10) Liability. Contractor shall be liable for any injury or damage occurring on account of the performance of its Work under this Contract. To the fullest extent permitted by law, Contractor agrees to defend, pay on behalf of, indemnify and hold harmless Owner, its elected and appointed officials, employees and volunteers and others working on behalf of Owner, against any and all claims, demands, suits or loss, including all costs connected therewith, and for any damages which may be asserted, claimed or recovered against or from the Owner, by reason of personal injury, including without limitation bodily injury or death, and/or property damage, which arises out of or is in any way connected or associated with this Contract, the performance of Work under this Contract, or other actions or inactions of the Contractor in connection with the Work performed.

11)  $ \underline{\text{Insurance}} $. This Contract is conditioned on the Contractor meeting the insurance requirements that is required in accordance with Exhibit B being satisfied by the Contractor with said insurance coverage to be maintained for the life of this Contract and the Owner being entitled to thirty (30) days written notice of any cancellations or changes.

12) Termination. Owner reserves the right to terminate the Contract upon thirty (30) days written notice. In the event this Contract is terminated prior to completion of Work, the Owner shall not be responsible to any further payments for work performed after the effective date of such termination, and shall pay Contractor for such work as has been completed and is eligible for payment under the terms of this Contract through the date of such termination.

## 13 )  $ \underline{\text{Miscellaneous}} $

a) The parties may execute this Contract in counterparts, each of which is deemed an original, but all of which together constitute one and the same Contract.

b) This Contract may be delivered by facsimile transmission, and facsimile copies of executed signature pages shall be binding as originals.

c) This Contract shall benefit and bind the parties and their respective successors, heirs, legal representatives and

<a id="p47"></a>

permitted assignees.

d) Neither the Owner nor the Contractor may assign this Contract without written consent of the other.

e) If any provision of this Contract is found to be illegal or unenforceable, the other provisions shall remain effective and enforceable to the greatest extent permitted by law.

f) This Contract shall be governed by Michigan law without regard to conflicts of law.

This Contract constitutes the entire agreement between the parties with respect to the Work and supersedes all prior written and oral agreements between the parties regarding the subject matter of this Contract.

## EXHIBIT A

City of South Lyon (Owner, aka "South Lyon") and Challenger Technologies, LLC (Contractor, aka "Challenger")

## Scope of Work (the Work):

Directional bore approximately 480' of one (1) 1.25" HDPE conduit with locating wire.

Bore from the northwest corner of the Police Department building to the east side of the Fire Department, then south to the pole by the parking lot. Set a vault over the existing conduit.

From the vault, bore over to the Fire Department building.

Route cable from Admin Building through existing conduit out to the vault. From the vault, route through the new conduit over to the Police Department. Attach a conduit to the wall and route the cable up into the attic space, then over to the IDF.

Route cable from Admin Building through existing conduit out to the vault. From the vault, route through the new conduit over to the Fire Department. Attach a conduit to the wall and route the cable up and enter above the ceiling, then over to the IDF.

Supply and install 1,000' of single-mode 12-strand fiber optic drop cable.

Supply and install one (1) 1RU fiber termination shelf, with two (2) 12-port S/C adapter panels.

Supply and install two (2) fiber termination boxes, with one (1) 12-port S/C adapter panel each.

Fusion splice all fiber strands.

Supply customer with as-built drawings and fiber documentation upon completion.

# EXHIBIT B INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS

The Contractor, and any and all of their subcontractors, shall not commence work under this contract until they have obtained the insurance required under this paragraph, and shall keep such insurance in force during the entire life of this contract. All coverage shall be with insurance companies licensed and admitted to do business in the State of Michigan and acceptable to the City of South Lyon. The requirements below should not be interpreted to limit the liability of the Contractor. All deductibles and SIRs are the responsibility of the Contractor.

The Contractor shall procure and maintain the following insurance coverage:

1.  $ \underline{\text{Workers' Compensation Insurance}} $, including Employers' Liability Coverage, in accordance with all applicable statutes of the State of Michigan.

2. Commercial General Liability Insurance on an “Occurrence Basis” with limits of liability not less than $1,000,000 per occurrence and $2,000,000 aggregate. Coverage shall include, but not limited to, the following: (A) Contractual Liability; (B) Products and Completed Operations; (C) Independent Contractors Coverage; (D) Broad Form General Liability Extensions or equivalent; (E) Explosion, Collapse, and Underground, if applicable.

3.  $ \underline{\text{Automobile Liability}} $, including Michigan No-Fault Coverages, with limits of liability not less than 1,000,000 per occurrence combined single limit for Bodily Injury, and Property Damage. Coverage shall include all owned vehicles, all non-owned vehicles, and all hired vehicles.

4. Additional Insured: Policy(ies) and coverages as described above, excluding Workers' Compensation Insurance, shall include an endorsement stating the following shall be Additional Insureds: The City of South Lyon, all elected and appointed officials, all employees and volunteers, agents, all boards, commissions, and/or authorities and board members, including employees and volunteers thereof. It is understood and agreed by naming the City of South Lyon as additional insured, coverage afforded is considered to be primary and any other insurance the City of South Lyon may have in effect shall be considered secondary and/or excess.

5. Cancellation Notice: Policy(ies), as described above, shall be endorsed to state the following: It is understood and agreed Thirty (30) days, Ten (10) days for non-payment of premium, Advance Written Notice of Cancellation, Non-Renewal, Reduction, and/or Material Change shall be sent to: City Manager Zelenak, City of South Lyon, 335 South Warren Street, South Lyon, MI 48178.

6.  $ \underline{\text{Proof of Insurance Coverage}} $: The Contractor shall provide the City of South Lyon at the time the contracts are returned by him/her for execution a Certificate of Insurance as well as the required endorsements. In lieu of required endorsements, a copy of the policy sections, where coverage is provided for additional insured and cancellation notice, may be acceptable. Copies of all policies mentioned above shall be furnished, if so requested.

Required liability limits may be obtained by using an Excess/Umbrella Liability policy in addition to the primary liability policy(ies). If coverage limits are satisfied by an Excess and/or Umbrella policy, coverage must follow form of the primary liability policy(ies), including but not limited to additional insured and primary/non-contributory coverage.

If any of the above coverages expire during the term of this contract, the Contractor shall deliver renewal certificates, endorsements, and/or policies to City of South Lyon at least ten (10) days prior to the expiration date.

Having our own fiber lines installed underground is more secure when handling sensitive information.5.) All fiber that is installed, can be used for any other IP phone or internet service used in the future.6.) If going with Comcast, installing these fiber lines will significantly reduce the cost from needing a fiber line to each building ($750 per building per month) to only needing one fiber line for all three buildings ($750 per month)</td><td>1.) Would have to be called when there is a problem with the fiber which creates minor additional costs. # AGENDA NOTE New Business # 4

MEETING DATE: August 24, 2020

PERSON PLACING ITEM ON AGENDA: City Manager

AGENDA TOPIC: Road Improvement Plan Discussion

EXPLANATION OF TOPIC: The City of South Lyon recently had a road conditions assessment performed (PASER Rating). This was done to not only assess the condition of our roads, but to also provide a cost estimate to improve the individual road segments.

Tonight we will have our first discussion on addressing the long term repair of our roads, including discussing revenues, expenditures, funding alternatives, and how to plan and move forward with determining a long-term solution to repair our roads.

MATERIALS ATTACHED AS SUPPORTING DOCUMENTS: Memo on Road the Improvement Plan, PASER Study, 2020-2021 Local and Major Road Revenue and expenditures with corresponding pie charts, maps, graphs, road condition photos and an example of a road cross section.

POSSIBLE COURSES OF ACTION: Discussion on the planning and moving forward with a City Road Improvement Plan.

SUGGESTED MOTION: None

<a id="p56"></a>
*[Page 56 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/8-24-20%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202108310733160#page=56)* (read by OCR; check the original)

## City of South Lyon

## Road Improvement Plan Discussion

<a id="p57"></a>
*[Page 57 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/8-24-20%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202108310733160#page=57)* (read by OCR; check the original)

As a result of completing a PASER study (a surface rating of our roads) the City of South Lyon has been able to estimate costs to repair roads within our community. As such we now have taken the first step to develop a long-term plan (10+ years) for funding repairs, maintenance and improvements of 23.5 miles of Local Roads and 4.5 miles of Major Roads in the City of South Lyon.

## Road Assessment

The attached PASER study within the Roadway Asset Management Plan, indicates the surface rating of our roads based on an evaluation process of each road within the community. The PASER study indicates that the majority of our roads are in poor condition. Included within the document is a projected 10-year average PASER rating for various funding levels to improve the roads. There is a map included that identifies the conditions of each of the road depicted in a particular color based upon the condition (1-10).

Road repairs take on many forms including: Crack Sealing, Patching, Mill and Overlay and Reconstruction

Attached also is a typical road cross-section that illustrates what is contained in an average road. (i.e. sand, gravel and pavement)

I have additionally attached examples of sample roads conditions within our community.

## Revenues / Expenditures

Attached is the itemized 2020-2021 road revenue / expenditures for Local and Major Streets. Its is outlined on the budget sheets and depicted in the pie chart.

## Funding Methods

The City receives the majority of its road revenue from ACT 51 funds. ACT 51 funding comes from revenues from gasoline and vehicle registration fees. The distribution is calculated using the most recent census population figures and the total miles of road we have in the community.

Other examples to fund road maintenance / repairs / improvements include:

County Road Funding – funding directly received from the county for a particular road. Tri Party Funds-which is a combination of City, County and RCOC funds for a specific improvement. SAD – Special Assessment Districts - which is a specific levy designed to recover the costs of improvement that benefits

<a id="p58"></a>
*[Page 58 of the original](https://www.southlyonmi.org/8-24-20%20Council%20Packet%20-%20online%20only.pdf?t=202108310733160#page=58)* (read by OCR; check the original)

the property. It is levied against the lands benefitting by the improvement. Federal Funds – funds directly to be used based upon the requirements of receiving the funds. General Fund-City tax dollars allocated in the budget to be used on city expenses. Bonds – A loan that is designated to pay for a particular improvement. Levied Road Millage – a voter approved millage by the residents.

A levy of (1) one mill on property would generate approximately $400,000 per year in additional revenue.

Currently we have the ability to levy one additional mill under our millage capped amount. Anything more would have to go before the vote of the electorate.

Bonding for roads is a means to start quickly on road repairs. With a bond you are able to start out with funding road improvements right away, instead of having to wait for taxes or other funds to be collected to pay for a project. However, any bond comes at a cost (interest expense). Bonding for a $1 million dollars for ten years will cost the city an extra $200,000 in bond costs for the life of the bond. Bonds are obtained to fund any SAD process as well.

## Infrastructure

When funding road repairs we must also look at the infrastructure that is to be included as part of any project including: storm sewer, curb and gutter, manholes, etc. And what might be below ground that should be addressed prior to paving (i.e. watermain and sanitary sewer lines)

## Conclusion

We have outlined some of the items that need to be discussed and determined in moving forward in developing the City's Road Improvement Plan. Internally we move forward with allocating time and funding to prepare data on road funding options and determine the pros and cons of each. We review road segments and group them together in paving projects based upon conditions of the road and funding availability. We review infrastructure that needs to be repaired or replaced as part of a prior to a paving project. Ensuring that its in the pipeline where it would be done in a timely manner before a road segment would be repaired/reconstructed. We report back periodically to City Council on the process via City Council agenda item. Where we will have pertinent discussions, make plans and decisions when necessary on moving the process forward. Ultimately, we have a long-term Road Improvement Plan that will outline the funding and the timeline for paving segments of road within the community.
